i < hex_len / 2; ++i) {\n   108→        unsigned int byte;\n   109→        if (sscanf(&hex[i * 2], \"%2x\", &byte) != 1)\n   110→            return -1;\n   111→        bytes_out[i] = (uint8_t)byte;\n   112→    }\n   113→    return (int)(hex_len / 2);\n   114→}\n   115→\n   116→/* HTTP parameter convenience ---------------------------------------------- */\n   117→static char *\n   118→get_query_param(struct evkeyvalq *params, const char *key)\n   119→{\n   120→    return (char *)evhttp_find_header(params, key);\n   121→}\n   122→\n   123→/* Reads the body of an evhttp_request into a null-terminated buffer.\n   124→ * The returned pointer must be freed by the caller. */\n   125→static char *\n   126→read_request_body(struct evhttp_request *req)\n   127→{\n   128→    struct evbuffer *buf = evhttp_request_get_input_buffer(req);\n   129→    size_t len           = evbuffer_get_length(buf);\n   130→    if (len == 0 || len > MAX_JSON_BODY) {\n   131→        return NULL;\n   132→    }\n   133→\n   134→    char *data = calloc(1, len + 1);\n   135→    if (!data)\n   136→        return NULL;\n   137→\n   138→    evbuffer_copyout(buf, data, len);\n   139→    return data;\n   140→}\n   141→\n   142→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   143→/*                                Key-Store                                  */\n   144→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   145→\n   146→/*\n   147→ * The keystore is a simple in-memory, thread-safe map:\n   148→ *      address (hex-encoded pubkey)  ->  wallet_entry_t\n   149→ *\n   150→ * WARNING: This is for demonstration only.  Never store private keys\n   151→ *          like this in production.\n   152→ */\n   153→\n   154→typedef struct wallet_entry {\n   155→    uint8_t  pubkey[crypto_sign_PUBLICKEYBYTES];\n   156→    uint8_t  seckey[crypto_sign_SECRETKEYBYTES];\n   157→    char     address[crypto_sign_PUBLICKEYBYTES * 2 + 1]; /* hex-encoded */\n   158→    UT_hash_handle hh; /* makes this structure hashable */\n   159→} wallet_entry_t;\n   160→\n   161→static wallet_entry_t *g_keystore = NULL;\n   162→static pthread_mutex_t g_keystore_lock = PTHREAD_MUTEX_INITIALIZER;\n   163→\n   164→static wallet_entry_t *\n   165→keystore_lookup(const char *address)\n   166→{\n   167→    wallet_entry_t *entry = NULL;\n   168→    pthread_mutex_lock(&g_keystore_lock);\n   169→    HASH_FIND_STR(g_keystore, address, entry);\n   170→    pthread_mutex_unlock(&g_keystore_lock);\n   171→    return entry;\n   172→}\n   173→\n   174→static int\n   175→keystore_insert(const uint8_t pubkey[crypto_sign_PUBLICKEYBYTES],\n   176→                const uint8_t seckey[crypto_sign_SECRETKEYBYTES],\n   177→                char **address_out)\n   178→{\n   179→    wallet_entry_t *entry = malloc(sizeof *entry);\n   180→    if (!entry)\n   181→        return -1;\n   182→\n   183→    memcpy(entry->pubkey, pubkey, crypto_sign_PUBLICKEYBYTES);\n   184→    memcpy(entry->seckey, seckey, crypto_sign_SECRETKEYBYTES);\n   185→\n   186→    bytes_to_hex(pubkey, crypto_sign_PUBLICKEYBYTES, entry->address);\n   187→\n   188→    pthread_mutex_lock(&g_keystore_lock);\n   189→    HASH_ADD_STR(g_keystore, address, entry);\n   190→    pthread_mutex_unlock(&g_keystore_lock);\n   191→\n   192→    if (address_out)\n   193→        *address_out = strdup(entry->address);\n   194→\n   195→    return 0;\n   196→}\n   197→\n   198→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   199→/*                           gRPC Stubbed Adapters                           */\n   200→/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n   201→\n   202→/* In production, the following functions would marshal protobuf messages\n   203→ * and call LedgerCore over the network. It provides:\n *\n *   • Wallet creation (Ed25519)\n *   • Balance look-ups (delegated to LedgerCore over gRPC)\n *   • Transaction signing\n *   • Transaction broadcast forwarding\n *\n * The service is intentionally stateless with the exception of an\n * in-memory, thread-safe key store that is meant only for demonstration\n * purposes.  Production deployments SHOULD replace the keystore with a\n * secure HSM or enclave-backed store.\n *\n * Build\n * -----\n * gcc -O2 -Wall -Wextra -pedantic -pthread \\\n *     proxy_server.c -o wallet_proxy \\\n *     -levent -lcjson -lsodium\n */\n\n#include <stdio.h>\n#include <stdlib.h>\n#include <string.h>\n#include <stdint.h>\n#include <signal.h>\n#include <time.h>\n#include <pthread.h>\n\n#include <event2/event.h>\n#include <event2/http.h>\n#include <event2/buffer.h>\n#include <event2/keyvalq_struct.h>\n\n#include <sodium.h>\n#include \"uthash.h\"          /* Header-only hash map: https://troydhanson.github.io/uthash/ */\n#include \"cjson/cJSON.h\"     /* Assumed to be available on include path */\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                                Configuration                               */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n#define LISTEN_ADDRESS      \"0.0.0.0\"\n#define LISTEN_PORT         8082\n#define SERVICE_VERSION     \"1.3.0\"\n\n#define JSON_CONTENT_TYPE   \"application/json; charset=utf-8\"\n#define MAX_JSON_BODY       (64 * 1024)   /* 64 KiB */\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                               Logging Macros                               */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n#define LOG_LEVEL_INFO  1\n#define LOG_LEVEL_WARN  2\n#define LOG_LEVEL_ERR   3\n\n#ifndef LOG_LEVEL\n#   define LOG_LEVEL LOG_LEVEL_INFO\n#endif\n\n#define LOG_PRINTF(level, fmt, ...)                                      \\\n    do {                                                                 \\\n        if ((level) >= LOG_LEVEL) {                                      \\\n            const char *_l = (level) == LOG_LEVEL_INFO ? \"INFO\" :        \\\n                             (level) == LOG_LEVEL_WARN ? \"WARN\" : \"ERROR\";\\\n            fprintf(stderr, \"[%s][%s:%d] \" fmt \"\\n\", _l,                 \\\n                    __FILE__, __LINE__, ##__VA_ARGS__);                  \\\n        }                                                                \\\n    } while (0)\n\n#define LOG_INFO(fmt, ...)  LOG_PRINTF(LOG_LEVEL_INFO, fmt, ##__VA_ARGS__)\n#define LOG_WARN(fmt, ...)  LOG_PRINTF(LOG_LEVEL_WARN, fmt, ##__VA_ARGS__)\n#define LOG_ERR(fmt, ...)   LOG_PRINTF(LOG_LEVEL_ERR,  fmt, ##__VA_ARGS__)\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                                 Utilities                                  */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/* Hex-encoding helpers ----------------------------------------------------- */\nstatic void\nbytes_to_hex(const uint8_t *bytes, size_t len, char *hex_out)\n{\n    static const char lut[] = \"0123456789abcdef\";\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < len; ++i) {\n        hex_out[i * 2]     = lut[(bytes[i] >> 4) & 0x0F];\n        hex_out[i * 2 + 1] = lut[ bytes[i]       & 0x0F];\n    }\n    hex_out[len * 2] = '\\0';\n}\n\nstatic int\nhex_to_bytes(const char *hex, uint8_t *bytes_out, size_t max_len)\n{\n    size_t hex_len = strlen(hex);\n    if (hex_len % 2 != 0 || hex_len / 2 > max_len)\n        return -1;\n\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < hex_len / 2; ++i) {\n        unsigned int byte;\n        if (sscanf(&hex[i * 2], \"%2x\", &byte) != 1)\n            return -1;\n        bytes_out[i] = (uint8_t)byte;\n    }\n    return (int)(hex_len / 2);\n}\n\n/* HTTP parameter convenience ---------------------------------------------- */\nstatic char *\nget_query_param(struct evkeyvalq *params, const char *key)\n{\n    return (char *)evhttp_find_header(params, key);\n}\n\n/* Reads the body of an evhttp_request into a null-terminated buffer.\n * The returned pointer must be freed by the caller. */\nstatic char *\nread_request_body(struct evhttp_request *req)\n{\n    struct evbuffer *buf = evhttp_request_get_input_buffer(req);\n    size_t len           = evbuffer_get_length(buf);\n    if (len == 0 || len > MAX_JSON_BODY) {\n        return NULL;\n    }\n\n    char *data = calloc(1, len + 1);\n    if (!data)\n        return NULL;\n\n    evbuffer_copyout(buf, data, len);\n    return data;\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                                Key-Store                                  */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/*\n * The keystore is a simple in-memory, thread-safe map:\n *      address (hex-encoded pubkey)  ->  wallet_entry_t\n *\n * WARNING: This is for demonstration only.  Never store private keys\n *          like this in production.\n */\n\ntypedef struct wallet_entry {\n    uint8_t  pubkey[crypto_sign_PUBLICKEYBYTES];\n    uint8_t  seckey[crypto_sign_SECRETKEYBYTES];\n    char     address[crypto_sign_PUBLICKEYBYTES * 2 + 1]; /* hex-encoded */\n    UT_hash_handle hh; /* makes this structure hashable */\n} wallet_entry_t;\n\nstatic wallet_entry_t *g_keystore = NULL;\nstatic pthread_mutex_t g_keystore_lock = PTHREAD_MUTEX_INITIALIZER;\n\nstatic wallet_entry_t *\nkeystore_lookup(const char *address)\n{\n    wallet_entry_t *entry = NULL;\n    pthread_mutex_lock(&g_keystore_lock);\n    HASH_FIND_STR(g_keystore, address, entry);\n    pthread_mutex_unlock(&g_keystore_lock);\n    return entry;\n}\n\nstatic int\nkeystore_insert(const uint8_t pubkey[crypto_sign_PUBLICKEYBYTES],\n                const uint8_t seckey[crypto_sign_SECRETKEYBYTES],\n                char **address_out)\n{\n    wallet_entry_t *entry = malloc(sizeof *entry);\n    if (!entry)\n        return -1;\n\n    memcpy(entry->pubkey, pubkey, crypto_sign_PUBLICKEYBYTES);\n    memcpy(entry->seckey, seckey, crypto_sign_SECRETKEYBYTES);\n\n    bytes_to_hex(pubkey, crypto_sign_PUBLICKEYBYTES, entry->address);\n\n    pthread_mutex_lock(&g_keystore_lock);\n    HASH_ADD_STR(g_keystore, address, entry);\n    pthread_mutex_unlock(&g_keystore_lock);\n\n    if (address_out)\n        *address_out = strdup(entry->address);\n\n    return 0;\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                           gRPC Stubbed Adapters                           */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/* In production, the following functions would marshal protobuf messages\n * and call LedgerCore over the network. 10'000) * 100'0000ULL;\n    LOG_INFO(\"ledger_get_balance(%s) -> %\" PRIu64, address, *balance_out);\n    return 0;\n}\n\nstatic int\nledger_broadcast_transaction(const char *signed_tx_hex)\n{\n All micro-services—Cryptograph,\n//      LedgerCore, MintFactory, GalleryGateway, DeFiGarden, OracleBridge,\n//      WalletProxy, and GovernanceHall—share this file to guarantee binary\n//      compatibility across language stacks (C, Go, Rust, TS, …).\n//\n//      The protocol is designed for high-throughput event streaming while still\n//      allowing fine-grained, request/response RPCs where appropriate.\n//      Multiplexed topic identifiers (topic_id) let a single bidirectional\n//      stream carry heterogeneous event types without sacrificing strong type\n//      safety.\n//\n//  Notes:\n//      1. All domain objects include an immutable uuid and a RFC-3339 timestamp.\n//      2. Blockchain “block_height” indexes finalized state on the roll-up\n//         layer.  A value of 0 means “pending / mem-pool”.                 ]:-)\n//\n//  Copyright © 2023-2024 The HoloCanvas Authors.\n//\n\npackage holocanvas.v1;\n\noption csharp_namespace  = \"HoloCanvas.Proto\";\noption java_package      = \"com.holocanvas.proto.v1\";\noption go_package        = \"github.com/holocanvas/proto/gen/go/holocanvas/v1;holocanvasv1\";\noption objc_class_prefix = \"HCX\";\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Google Well-Known Types\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\nimport \"google/protobuf/any.proto\";\nimport \"google/protobuf/struct.proto\";\nimport \"google/protobuf/timestamp.proto\";\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// ENUMERATIONS\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nenum ArtifactState {\n    ARTIFACT_STATE_UNSPECIFIED  = 0;\n    ARTIFACT_STATE_DRAFT        = 1;\n    ARTIFACT_STATE_CURATED      = 2;\n    ARTIFACT_STATE_AUCTION      = 3;\n    ARTIFACT_STATE_FRACTIONAL   = 4;\n    ARTIFACT_STATE_STAKED       = 5;\n    ARTIFACT_STATE_ARCHIVED     = 6;\n}\n\nenum MediaKind {\n    MEDIA_KIND_UNSPECIFIED = 0;\n    MEDIA_KIND_SHADER      = 1;   // GLSL / WGSL fragment\n    MEDIA_KIND_AUDIO       = 2;   // WebAudio / MIDI\n    MEDIA_KIND_METADATA    = 3;   // JSON-LD, IPFS CID, etc.\n}\n\nenum GovernanceAction {\n    GOVERNANCE_ACTION_UNSPECIFIED = 0;\n    GOVERNANCE_ACTION_MINT_RIGHTS = 1;\n    GOVERNANCE_ACTION_CURATE      = 2;\n    GOVERNANCE_ACTION_EVOLVE      = 3;\n    GOVERNANCE_ACTION_FREEZE      = 4;\n    GOVERNANCE_ACTION_BURN        = 5;\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// CORE DOMAIN OBJECTS\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n// A cryptographically provable identity (wallet, service, or DAO).\nmessage Principal {\n    string  did           = 1;  // W3C Decentralized Identifier, e.g. \"did:key:…\"\n    bytes   public_key    = 2;  // Raw secp256k1 key bytes\n    string  alias         = 3;  // Human-friendly label\n}\n\n// Represents a single layer of media inside an Artifact.\nmessage MediaLayer {\n    string      uuid            = 1;  // Layer UUID\n    MediaKind   kind            = 2;\n    string      content_uri     = 3;  // IPFS / Arweave / HTTPS URI\n    bytes       content_hash    = 4;  // SHA-256 digest of raw bytes\n    uint32      ordinal         = 5;  // Z-index for compositing\n    google.protobuf.Timestamp created_at = 6;\n}\n\n// Immutable recipe captured at mint-time.\nmessage ArtifactRecipe {\n    string                        uuid          = 1;\n    repeated MediaLayer           layers        = 2;\n    google.protobuf.Struct        metadata      = 3;  // Arbitrary creator-supplied JSON\n    string                        factory_addr  = 4;  // On-chain smart-contract address\n    google.protobuf.Timestamp     created_at    = 5;\n}\n\n// NFT Artifact tracked on-chain.\nmessage Artifact {\n    string               uuid           = 1;\n    ArtifactState        state          = 2;\n    ArtifactRecipe       recipe         = 3;\n    Principal            owner          = 4;\n    uint64               block_height   = 5;  // 0 => pending\n    google.protobuf.Timestamp updated_at = 6;\n}\n\n// Generic financial amount in Wei, USD-cents, etc.\nmessage Amount {\n    string  currency = 1; // e.g. \"wei\", \"usdc\"\n    uint64  value    = 2;\n}\n\n// Bid placed during Auction state.\nmessage Bid {\n    string                   uuid         = 1;\n    string                   artifact_uuid= 2;\n    Principal                bidder       = 3;\n    Amount                   amount       = 4;\n    google.protobuf.Timestamp placed_at   = 5;\n}\n\n// Staking position.\nmessage Stake {\n    string                   uuid          = 1;\n    string                   artifact_uuid = 2;\n    Principal                staker        = 3;\n    Amount                   amount        = 4;\n    uint32                   lock_days     = 5;\n    google.protobuf.Timestamp staked_at    = 6;\n}\n\n// Governance proposal document.\nmessage GovernanceProposal {\n    string                       uuid            = 1;\n    string                       artifact_uuid   = 2;\n    GovernanceAction             action          = 3;\n    google.protobuf.Struct       params          = 4;  // action-specific\n    Principal                    proposer        = 5;\n    google.protobuf.Timestamp    submitted_at    = 6;\n    repeated GovernanceVote      votes           = 7;\n}\n\n// Individual vote.\nmessage GovernanceVote {\n    Principal                    voter        = 1;\n    bool                         approve      = 2;\n    google.protobuf.Timestamp    cast_at      = 3;\n}\n\n// Data injected by external oracle feeds.\nmessage OracleEvent {\n    string                       uuid          = 1;\n    string                       source        = 2; // e.g. \"openweather\"\n    google.protobuf.Struct       payload       = 3;\n    google.protobuf.Timestamp    observed_at   = 4;\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// TRANSACTION ENVELOPE\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n// Unified event wrapper for streaming across Kafka / gRPC.\nmessage CanvasEvent {\n    string   uuid        = 1;\n    oneof    body {\n        Artifact            artifact              = 10;\n        Bid                 bid                   = 11;\n        Stake               stake                 = 12;\n        GovernanceProposal  proposal              = 13;\n        OracleEvent         oracle                = 14;\n    }\n    // Topic to aid in routing through pub/sub fabric.\n    string   topic_id    = 20; // e.g. \"artifact.{uuid}\" or \"governance.global\"\n    uint64   block_height= 21;\n    google.protobuf.Timestamp emitted_at = 22;\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// SERVICE APIs\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n// 1. Factory Pattern – composable mint pipeline.\nservice MintFactory {\n    // Mint a brand-new Artifact from a recipe.\n    rpc Mint(ArtifactRecipe) returns (Artifact);\n\n    // Retrieve an existing Artifact (regardless of state).\n    rpc GetArtifact(GetArtifactRequest) returns (Artifact);\n\n    // Subscribe to live state transitions for a specific Artifact.\n    rpc WatchArtifact(WatchArtifactRequest) returns (stream CanvasEvent);\n}\n\nmessage GetArtifactRequest {\n    string artifact_uuid = 1;\n}\n\nmessage WatchArtifactRequest {\n    string artifact_uuid = 1;\n}\n\n// 2. LedgerCore – canonical blockchain interface (light wrapper around rollup node).\nservice LedgerCore {\n    // Append a signed transaction to the roll-up mem-pool.\n    rpc SubmitEvent(CanvasEvent) returns (SubmitEventResponse);\n\n    // Stream finalized blocks for indexing or archival.\n    rpc StreamBlocks(StreamBlocksRequest) returns (stream CanvasEvent);\n}\n\nmessage SubmitEventResponse {\n    string  tx_hash      = 1;\n    uint64  mempool_seq  = 2;\n}\n\nmessage StreamBlocksRequest {\n    uint64 from_block = 1;\n}\n\n// 3. DeFiGarden – staking & fractionalization hub.\nservice DeFiGarden {\n    rpc StakeArtifact(Stake) returns (Stake);\n    rpc Unstake(UnstakeRequest) returns (Stake);\n    rpc WatchStakes(WatchStakesRequest) returns (stream Stake);\n}\n\nmessage UnstakeRequest {\n    string stake_uuid = 1;\n}\n\nmessage WatchStakesRequest {\n    string artifact_uuid = 1;\n}\n\n// 4. GovernanceHall – DAO governance portal.\nservice GovernanceHall {\n    rpc Propose(GovernanceProposal) returns (GovernanceProposal);\n    rpc Vote(GovernanceVote) returns (GovernanceProposal);\n    rpc WatchGovernance(WatchGovernanceRequest) returns (stream GovernanceProposal);\n}\n\nmessage WatchGovernanceRequest {\n    string artifact_uuid = 1; // empty = global stream\n}\n\n// 5. OracleBridge – gateway for external data feeds.\nservice OracleBridge {\n    rpc InjectOracleData(OracleEvent) returns (OracleEvent);\n    rpc WatchOracle(WatchOracleRequest) returns (stream OracleEvent);\n}\n\nmessage WatchOracleRequest {\n    string source = 1; // empty = all\n}\n\n// 6. WalletProxy – abstraction layer for user wallets.\nservice WalletProxy {\n    rpc SignAndSubmit(SignedPayload) returns (SubmitEventResponse);\n    rpc GetBalance(GetBalanceRequest) returns (Amount);\n}\n\nmessage SignedPayload {\n    bytes  payload   = 1;\n    bytes  signature = 2;\n}\n\nmessage GetBalanceRequest {\n    Principal account = 1;\n}\n\n// 7. GalleryGateway – read-only aggregation for UI/REST.\nservice GalleryGateway {\n    rpc ListArtifacts(ListArtifactsRequest) returns (stream Artifact);\n}\n\nmessage ListArtifactsRequest {\n    ArtifactState state_filter = 1; // UNSPECIFIED = all\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// BACKWARD-COMPATIBILITY NOTES\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// • Whenever you need to remove a field, use the “reserved” keyword to prevent\n//   accidental reuse. rd_kafka_topic_partition_list_destroy(listener->topics);\n\n    free(listener);\n}\n\n/* ----  Thread routine ---------------------------------------------------- */\n\nstatic void *listener_thread(void *arg) {\n    EventListener *listener = arg;\n    signal(SIGINT, sig_handler);\n    signal(SIGTERM, sig_handler);\n\n    while (listener->running && !g_shutdown) {\n        rd_kafka_message_t *rkmsg =\n            rd_kafka_consumer_poll(listener->rk, 100 /* ms */);\n\n        if (!rkmsg)\n            continue; /* timeout – poll again */\n\n        if (rkmsg->err) {\n   rkmsg->partition, rkmsg->offset);\n            } else {\n                LOG_WARN(\"Consume error: %s\",\n                         rd_kafka_message_errstr(rkmsg));\n            }\n            rd_kafka_message_destroy(rkmsg);\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        /* Dispatch user payload */\n        if (dispatch_event(listener,\n                           (const char *)rkmsg->payload,\n                           rkmsg->len) != 0) {\n            LOG_WARN(\"Failed to dispatch event (offset %\"PRId64\")\",\n                     rkmsg->offset);\n        }\n\n        rd_kafka_message_destroy(rkmsg);\n    }\n\n    rd_kafka_consumer_close(listener->rk);\n    return NULL;\n}\n\n/* ----  Event dispatch ---------------------------------------------------- */\n\nstatic int dispatch_event(EventListener *listener,\n                          const char *payload, size_t len) {\n    json_error_t jerr;\n    json_t *root = json_loadb(payload, len, 0, &jerr);\n    if (!root) {\n        LOG_WARN(\"JSON parse failed: %s (line %d)\", jerr.text, jerr.line);\n        return -1;\n    }\n\n    json_t *type = json_object_get(root, \"type\");\n    if listener->strategy->on_event(listener->strategy, &evt);\n    if (rc != 0)\n        LOG_WARN(\"Strategy returned non-zero (%d) for event %s\", rc, etype);\n\n    json_decref(root);\n    return rc;\n}\n```\n","numLines":344,"startLine":1,"totalLines":344}}}

329→pos_strategy_select_proposer(const uint8_t prev_block_hash[crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES],\n   330→                             wallet_addr_t out_addr)\n   331→{\n   332→ * -----------\n   421→ * Perform stake-weighted random election using libsodium's crypto_core_ed25519.\n   422→ * The VRF simulates a beacon: H = Hash(prev_hash || validator_pub || epoch).\n   423→ * Each validator computes H and compares it with a threshold derived from\n   424→ * stake/total.  The smallest valid H wins.  For centralised selection (this\n   425→ * service) we compute H for all candidates.\n   426→ */\n   427→static int\n   428→vrf_elect(const pos_ctx_t *ctx,\n   429→          const uint8_t prev_block_hash[crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES],\n   430→          wallet_addr_t out_selected)\n   431→{\n   432→    uint8_t min_hash[crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES];\n   433→    bool    selected = false;\n   434→\n   435→    for (size_t i = 0; i < ctx->v_len; ++i) {\n   436→        const validator_entry_t *v = &ctx->validators[i];\n   437→        if (v->jailed || v->stake == 0)\n   438→            continue;\n   439→\n   440→        /* Beacon: hash(prev_hash | validator_pub | epoch_le) */\n   441→        uint8_t buf[crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES + WALLET_ADDR_LEN + sizeof(uint64_t)];\n   442→        size_t  off = 0;\n   443→        memcpy(buf + off, prev_block_hash, crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES); off += crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES;\n   444→        memcpy(buf + off, v->address, WALLET_ADDR_LEN);               off += WALLET_ADDR_LEN;\n   445→        uint64_t epoch_le = htole64(ctx->current_epoch);\n   446→        memcpy(buf + off, &epoch_le, sizeof(epoch_le));\n   447→\n   448→        uint8_t (!selected || memcmp(h, min_hash, 16) < 0) {\n   453→            memcpy(min_hash, h, sizeof(min_hash));\n   454→            memcpy(out_selected, v->address, WALLET_ADDR_LEN);\n   455→            selected = true;\n   456→        }\n   457→    }\n   458→\n   459→    if (!selected)\n   460→        return -1;\n   461→\n   462→    return 0;\n   463→}\n   464→\n   465→\n   466→/*\n   467→ * 472→static int\n   473→apply_block_rewards(pos_ctx_t *ctx,\n   474→                    const wallet_addr_t proposer,\n   475→                    uint64_t block_fees)\n   476→{\n   477→    static const 0) {\n   482→        pthread_rwlock_unlock(&ctx->rwlock);\n   483→        return -1;\n   484→    }\n   485→\n   486→    validator_entry_t *v = &ctx->validators[idx];\n   487→    v->rewards += The strategy is loaded at runtime through a\n *   Strategy-Pattern interface (see pos_strategy.h) and receives a stream of\n *   ledger events via the internal Event-Bus.  The PoS module is responsible\n *   for:\n *\n *     • Managing validator stake tables\n *     • Electing the next block proposer with a stake-weighted VRF\n *     • Verifying block signatures and stake lock-ups\n *     • Distributing block rewards and slashing misbehaving validators\n *\n *   The implementation is production-grade and is optimised for readability\n *   and robustness rather than raw performance.  Critical sections are\n *   protected by pthread mutexes; cryptographic operations rely on libsodium.\n *\n *   NOTE: The surrounding infrastructure (ledger storage, event bus, etc.)\n *         is mocked by forward declarations to keep this file self-contained.\n *         In the full code-base these come from their respective headers.\n *\n * License:\n *   Apache-2.0\n */\n\n#include <assert.h>\n#include <errno.h>\n#include <inttypes.h>\n#include <sodium.h>\n#include <stdbool.h>\n#include <stdint.h>\n#include <stdio.h>\n#include <stdlib.h>\n#include <string.h>\n#include <pthread.h>\n#include \"pos_strategy.h\"  /* Public interface */\n#include \"../include/crypto_utils.h\"      /* crypto_sign_block(), hash(), … */\n#include \"../include/event_bus.h\"         /* event_bus_publish(), … */\n#include \"../include/ledger_storage.h\"    /* ledger_get_balance(), … */\n#include \"../include/logger.h\"            /* HC_LOG_DEBUG(), … */\n\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------------------- *\n *                              Local  Types                                 *\n * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/* Validator entry kept in memory; periodically snapshotted to disk */\ntypedef struct validator_entry_s\n{\n    wallet_addr_t  address;        /* 32-byte public key (bech32-decoded) */\n    uint64_t       stake;          /* Currently bonded stake              */\n    uint64_t       locked_until;   /* Epoch height until which stake is locked */\n    uint64_t       rewards;        /* Pending rewards (lazy withdrawn)    */\n    bool           jailed;         /* true when validator is slashed      */\n} validator_entry_t;\n\n/* Main PoS context – one per service instance */\ntypedef struct pos_ctx_s\n{\n    validator_entry_t *validators; /* Dynamic array                       */\n    size_t             v_len;      /* Elements in the array               */\n    size_t             v_cap;      /* Capacity                            */\n\n    uint64_t           total_stake;/* Aggregate bonded stake              */\n    uint64_t           current_epoch;\n    event_bus_t       *bus;        /* Weak ref to global Event-Bus        */\n\n    pthread_rwlock_t   rwlock;     /* Protects validators & total_stake   */\n} pos_ctx_t;\n\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------------------- *\n *                       Forward Declarations / Helpers                      *\n * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\nstatic int      ensure_capacity(pos_ctx_t *ctx);\nstatic ssize_t  find_validator(pos_ctx_t *ctx, const wallet_addr_t addr);\nstatic int      add_validator(pos_ctx_t *ctx,\n                              const wallet_addr_t addr,\n                              uint64_t stake);\n\nstatic int      vrf_elect(const pos_ctx_t *ctx,\n                          const uint8_t previous_block_hash[crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES],\n                          wallet_addr_t out_selected);\n\nstatic int      apply_block_rewards(pos_ctx_t *ctx,\n                                    const wallet_addr_t proposer,\n                                    uint64_t block_fees);\n\nstatic int      slash_validator(pos_ctx_t *ctx,\n                                const wallet_addr_t address,\n                                const char *reason);\n\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------------------- *\n *                       Public  Interface  (Strategy)                       *\n * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\nstatic pos_ctx_t g_ctx;  /* Singleton instance – strategy is process-local */\n\n\n/*\n * pos_strategy_init()\n * -------------------\n * Initialise libsodium (if necessary), internal state and subscribe to the\n * Event-Bus streams required by the PoS module.\n */\nint\npos_strategy_init(event_bus_t *bus)\n{\n    if (sodium_init() == -1) {\n        fprintf(stderr, \"[PoS] libsodium init failed\\n\");\n        return -1;\n    }\n\n    memset(&g_ctx, 0, sizeof(g_ctx));\n    g_ctx.bus = bus;\n    pthread_rwlock_init(&g_ctx.rwlock, NULL);\n\n    /* Pre-allocate validator table with a sane default */\n    g_ctx.v_cap = 64;\n    g_ctx.validators = calloc(g_ctx.v_cap, sizeof(validator_entry_t));\n    if (!g_ctx.validators) {\n        perror(\"[PoS] calloc validators\");\n        return -1;\n    }\n\n    /* Subscribe to ledger events (mocked) */\n    event_bus_subscribe(bus, EVENT_STAKE_BOND,  pos_strategy_on_event);\n    event_bus_subscribe(bus, EVENT_STAKE_UNBOND,pos_strategy_on_event);\n    event_bus_subscribe(bus, EVENT_NEW_BLOCK,   pos_strategy_on_event);\n\n    HC_LOG_INFO(\"[PoS] strategy initialised\");\n    return 0;\n}\n\n\n/*\n * pos_strategy_shutdown()\n * -----------------------\n * Flush any in-memory state to durable storage and cleanup allocations.\n */\nvoid\npos_strategy_shutdown(void)\n{\n    pthread_rwlock_wrlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n\n    /* In a real implementation we would persist validator snapshots here */\n\n    free(g_ctx.validators);\n    g_ctx.validators = NULL;\n    g_ctx.v_len      = 0;\n    g_ctx.v_cap      = 0;\n    g_ctx.total_stake= 0;\n\n    pthread_rwlock_unlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n    pthread_rwlock_destroy(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n\n    HC_LOG_INFO(\"[PoS] strategy shut down\");\n}\n\n\n/*\n * pos_strategy_on_event()\n * -----------------------\n * Central entry point for Event-Bus callbacks.  Handles three primary event\n * classes: stake bond/unbond, and new block arrival.\n */\nvoid\npos_strategy_on_event(const event_t *ev)\n{\n    switch (ev->type) {\n\n    case EVENT_STAKE_BOND:\n        pos_strategy_handle_bond((const stake_event_t *)ev->payload);\n        break;\n\n    case EVENT_STAKE_UNBOND:\n        pos_strategy_handle_unbond((const stake_event_t *)ev->payload);\n        break;\n\n    case EVENT_NEW_BLOCK:\n        pos_strategy_handle_block((const block_event_t *)ev->payload);\n        break;\n\n    default:\n        HC_LOG_WARN(\"[PoS] unknown event type: %d\", (int)ev->type);\n    }\n}\n\n\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------------------- *\n *                          Event  Handlers                                  *\n * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/*\n * Handle stake bonding.  Updates validator table and total stake.\n */\nvoid\npos_strategy_handle_bond(const stake_event_t *ev)\n{\n    int rc;\n    pthread_rwlock_wrlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n\n    ssize_t idx = find_validator(&g_ctx, ev->staker);\n    if (idx < 0) {\n        /* New validator */\n        rc = add_validator(&g_ctx, ev->staker, ev->amount);\n        if (rc != 0) {\n            HC_LOG_ERROR(\"[PoS] failed to add validator\");\n            pthread_rwlock_unlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n            return;\n        }\n    } else {\n        validator_entry_t *v = &g_ctx.validators[idx];\n        v->stake += ev->amount;\n        v->locked_until = g_ctx.current_epoch + POS_MIN_LOCK_EPOCHS;\n    }\n\n    g_ctx.total_stake += ev->amount;\n    pthread_rwlock_unlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n\n    HC_LOG_DEBUG(\"[PoS] bonded %\" PRIu64 \" to validator %.8s…, total=%\" PRIu64,\n                 ev->amount, ev->staker, g_ctx.total_stake);\n}\n\n\n/*\n * Handle stake unbonding request.  Stake is not immediately released: we place\n * it in a cooldown queue (omitted for brevity) and unlock once the holding\n * period expires.\n */\nvoid\npos_strategy_handle_unbond(const stake_event_t *ev)\n{\n    pthread_rwlock_wrlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n\n    ssize_t idx = find_validator(&g_ctx, ev->staker);\n    if (idx < 0) {\n        HC_LOG_WARN(\"[PoS] unbond from unknown validator %.8s…\", ev->staker);\n        pthread_rwlock_unlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n        return;\n    }\n\n    validator_entry_t *v = &g_ctx.validators[idx];\n    if (v->stake < ev->amount) {\n        HC_LOG_ERROR(\"[PoS] unbond exceeds stake\");\n        pthread_rwlock_unlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n        return;\n    }\n\n    v->stake -= ev->amount;\n    g_ctx.total_stake -= ev->amount;\n\n    /* TODO: Push to cooldown queue for delayed release */\n\n    pthread_rwlock_unlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n\n    HC_LOG_DEBUG(\"[PoS] unbonded %\" PRIu64 \" from validator %.8s…, remaining=%\"\n                 PRIu64, ev->amount, ev->staker, v->stake);\n}\n\n\n/*\n * Handle new block arrival.  Verify proposer signature and stake, distribute\n * fees and reward, then increment epoch if necessary.\n */\nvoid\npos_strategy_handle_block(const block_event_t *ev)\n{\n    const block_t *blk = &ev->block;\n\n    /* 1. Verify signature */\n    if (crypto_verify_block(blk) != 0) {\n        HC_LOG_ERROR(\"[PoS] invalid block signature\");\n        /* TODO: Reject block on the ledger pipeline */\n        return;\n    }\n\n    /* 2. Ensure proposer is valid and not jailed */\n    pthread_rwlock_rdlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n    ssize_t idx = find_validator(&g_ctx, blk->proposer);\n    if (idx < 0) {\n        pthread_rwlock_unlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n        HC_LOG_ERROR(\"[PoS] proposer %.8s… not in validator set\", blk->proposer);\n        return;\n    }\n\n    const validator_entry_t *v = &g_ctx.validators[idx];\n    if (v->jailed) {\n        pthread_rwlock_unlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n        HC_LOG_ERROR(\"[PoS] jailed proposer %.8s…\", blk->proposer);\n        /* Slash attempt */\n        slash_validator(&g_ctx, blk->proposer, \"Proposed block while jailed\");\n        return;\n    }\n    pthread_rwlock_unlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n\n    /* 3. Apply rewards */\n    if (apply_block_rewards(&g_ctx, blk->proposer, blk->fees) != 0) {\n        HC_LOG_ERROR(\"[PoS] reward distribution failed\");\n    }\n\n    /* 4. Advance epoch if block boundary crossed */\n    if (blk->height % POS_BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH == 0) {\n        g_ctx.current_epoch++;\n        HC_LOG_INFO(\"[PoS] epoch advanced to %\" PRIu64, g_ctx.current_epoch);\n    }\n}\n\n\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------------------- *\n *                    Consensus / Validator Selection                        *\n * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/*\n * pos_strategy_select_proposer()\n * ------------------------------\n * Called by the block-production pipeline to pick the next validator who\n * is eligible to create a block.  Uses a VRF to achieve randomness that\n * can be independently verified by all peers.\n *\n * Returns 0 on success and fills `out_addr`.\n */\nint\npos_strategy_select_proposer(const uint8_t prev_block_hash[crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES],\n                             wallet_addr_t out_addr)\n{\n    pthread_rwlock_rdlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n    if (g_ctx.total_stake == 0 || g_ctx.v_len == 0) {\n        pthread_rwlock_unlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n        HC_LOG_ERROR(\"[PoS] no stake available for selection\");\n        return -1;\n    }\n\n    int rc = vrf_elect(&g_ctx, prev_block_hash, out_addr);\n    pthread_rwlock_unlock(&g_ctx.rwlock);\n\n    return rc;\n}\n\n\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------------------- *\n *                        Internal helper functions                          *\n * ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/*\n * ensure_capacity()\n * -----------------\n * Grow the validator array if necessary.\n */\nstatic int\nensure_capacity(pos_ctx_t *ctx)\n{\n    if (ctx->v_len < ctx->v_cap)\n        return 0;\n\n    size_t new_cap = ctx->v_cap * 2;\n    validator_entry_t *tmp = realloc(ctx->validators,\n                                     new_cap * sizeof(validator_entry_t));\n    if (!tmp) {\n        perror(\"[PoS] realloc validators\");\n        return -1;\n    }\n    ctx->validators = tmp;\n    ctx->v_cap = new_cap;\n    return 0;\n}\n\n\n/*\n * find_validator()\n * ----------------\n * Return the index of a validator in the array, or ‑1 if not present.\n * Linear search is acceptable for few hundred validators; for larger sets an\n * ordered map should be used (omitted for brevity).\n */\nstatic ssize_t\nfind_validator(pos_ctx_t *ctx, const wallet_addr_t addr)\n{\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < ctx->v_len; ++i) {\n        if (memcmp(ctx->validators[i].address, addr, WALLET_ADDR_LEN) == 0)\n            return (ssize_t)i;\n    }\n    return -1;\n}\n\n\n/*\n * add_validator()\n * ---------------\n * Append a new validator to the table.\n */\nstatic int\nadd_validator(pos_ctx_t *ctx,\n              const wallet_addr_t addr,\n              uint64_t stake)\n{\n    if (ensure_capacity(ctx) != 0)\n        return -1;\n\n    validator_entry_t *v = &ctx->validators[ctx->v_len++];\n    memset(v, 0, sizeof(*v));\n    memcpy(v->address, addr, WALLET_ADDR_LEN);\n    v->stake        = stake;\n    v->locked_until = ctx->current_epoch + POS_MIN_LOCK_EPOCHS;\n    v->jailed       = false;\n\n    HC_LOG_INFO(\"[PoS] new validator %.8s…, stake=%\" PRIu64,\n                addr, stake);\n    return 0;\n}\n\n\n/*\n * vrf_elect()\n * -----------\n * Perform stake-weighted random election using libsodium's crypto_core_ed25519.\n * The VRF simulates a beacon: H = Hash(prev_hash || validator_pub || epoch).\n * Each validator computes H and compares it with a threshold derived from\n * stake/total.  The smallest valid H wins.  For centralised selection (this\n * service) we compute H for all candidates.\n */\nstatic int\nvrf_elect(const pos_ctx_t *ctx,\n          const uint8_t prev_block_hash[crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES],\n          wallet_addr_t out_selected)\n{\n    uint8_t min_hash[crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES];\n    bool    selected = false;\n\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < ctx->v_len; ++i) {\n        const validator_entry_t *v = &ctx->validators[i];\n        if (v->jailed || v->stake == 0)\n            continue;\n\n        /* Beacon: hash(prev_hash | validator_pub | epoch_le) */\n        uint8_t buf[crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES + WALLET_ADDR_LEN + sizeof(uint64_t)];\n        size_t  off = 0;\n        memcpy(buf + off, prev_block_hash, crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES); off += crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES;\n        memcpy(buf + off, v->address, WALLET_ADDR_LEN);               off += WALLET_ADDR_LEN;\n        uint64_t epoch_le = htole64(ctx->current_epoch);\n        memcpy(buf + off, &epoch_le, sizeof(epoch_le));\n\n        uint8_t h[crypto_hash_sha256_BYTES];\n        crypto_hash_sha256(h, buf, sizeof(buf));\n\n        /* Convert leading 16 bytes to a 128-bit integer for comparison */\n        if (!selected || memcmp(h, min_hash, 16) < 0) {\n            memcpy(min_hash, h, sizeof(min_hash));\n            memcpy(out_selected, v->address, WALLET_ADDR_LEN);\n            selected = true;\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (!selected)\n        return -1;\n\n    return 0;\n}\n\n\n/*\n * apply_block_rewards()\n * ---------------------\n * Credit proposer with block reward plus fees, and distribute a portion to\n * delegators (omitted for brevity).\n */\nstatic int\napply_block_rewards(pos_ctx_t *ctx,\n                    const wallet_addr_t proposer,\n                    uint64_t block_fees)\n{\n    static const uint64_t BASE_REWARD = 5 * HC_COINS; /* 5 tokens per block */\n\n    pthread_rwlock_wrlock(&ctx->rwlock);\n    ssize_t idx = find_validator(ctx, proposer);\n    if (idx < 0) {\n        pthread_rwlock_unlock(&ctx->rwlock);\n        return -1;\n    }\n\n    validator_entry_t *v = &ctx->validators[idx];\n    v->rewards += BASE_REWARD + block_fees;\n    pthread_rwlock_unlock(&ctx->rwlock);\n\n    /* Emit reward event */\n    reward_event_t rev = {\n        .validator = {0},\n        .amount    = BASE_REWARD + block_fees\n    };\n    memcpy(rev.validator, proposer, WALLET_ADDR_LEN);\n    event_t ev = {\n        .type    = EVENT_REWARD,\n        .payload = &rev\n    };\n    event_bus_publish(ctx->bus, &ev);\n\n    HC_LOG_DEBUG(\"[PoS] rewarded %.8s… with %\" PRIu64,\n                 proposer, rev.amount);\n    return 0;\n}\n\n\n/*\n * slash_validator()\n * -----------------\n * Slash a validator for misbehaviour by burning a percentage of its stake and\n * jailing it for several epochs.\n */\nstatic int\nslash_validator(pos_ctx_t *ctx,\n                const wallet_addr_t address,\n                const char *reason)\n{\n    pthread_rwlock_wrlock(&ctx->rwlock);\n    ssize_t idx = find_validator(ctx, address);\n    if (idx < 0) {\n        pthread_rwlock_unlock(&ctx->rwlock);\n        return -1;\n    }\n\n    validator_entry_t *v = &ctx->validators[idx];\n    uint64_t penalty = v->stake / POS_SLASH_FRACTION;\n    v->stake -= penalty;\n    v->jailed = true;\n\n    ctx->total_stake -= penalty;\n    pthread_rwlock_unlock(&ctx->rwlock);\n\n    HC_LOG_WARN(\"[PoS] validator %.8s… slashed by %\" PRIu64 \" (%s)\",\n                address, penalty, reason);\n\n    /* Emit slashing event */\n    slash_event_t sev = {\n        .validator = {0},\n        .amount    = penalty,\n        .reason    = {0}\n    };\n    memcpy(sev.validator, address, WALLET_ADDR_LEN);\n    strncpy(sev.reason, reason, sizeof(sev.reason)-1);\n\n    event_t ev = {\n        .type    = EVENT_SLASH,\n        .payload = &sev\n    };\n    event_bus_publish(ctx->bus, &ev);\n\n    return 0;\n}\n","numLines":554,"startLine":1,"totalLines":554}}}

cJSON_CreateObject();\n    if (!root) {\n        pthread_mutex_unlock(&rc->lock);\n        return rc_log_err(RC_ERR_JSON, \"failed to create JSON root\");\n    }\n\n    cJSON_AddStringToObject(root, \"artifact_id\", rc->artifact_id);\n    cJSON_AddStringToObject(root, \"author_wallet\", rc->author_wallet);\n\n    char iso8601[32] = {0};\n    time_t now = time(NULL);\n    strftime(iso8601, sizeof FRAG_METADATA: cJSON_AddStringToObject(entry, \"type\", \"metadata\"); break;\n            default:            cJSON_AddStringToObject(entry, \"type\", \"unknown\");  break;\n        }\n        /* b64_len = buffer_ptr->length;\n            }\n            BIO_free_all(b64f);\n        }\n\n        if (!b64) {\n            cJSON_Delete(root);\n            pthread_mutex_unlock(&rc->lock);\n            return rc_log_err(RC_ERR_OOM, \"base64 encoding failed\");\n        }\n\n        cJSON_AddStringToObject(entry, \"content_b64\", b64);\n        free(b64);\n        cJSON_AddItemToArray(frag_arr, entry);\n    }\n\n    char *json_text = cJSON_PrintUnformatted(root);\n    cJSON_Delete(root);\n    if (!json_text) {\n        pthread_mutex_unlock(&rc->lock);\n        return rc_log_err(RC_ERR_JSON, \"cJSON_Print failed\");\n    }\n\n    /* -------------------- Compute SHA-256 -------------------- */\n    unsigned char digest[SHA256_DIGEST_LENGTH];\n    SHA256((unsigned char *)json_text, strlen(json_text), digest);\n\n    for (int i = 0; i < SHA256_DIGEST_LENGTH; ++i)\n        sprintf(rc->sha256_hex + (i * 2), \"%02x\", digest[i]);\n\n    /* -------------------- Persist to storage -------------------- */\n    if (mkdir(storage_path, 0755) != 0 && errno != EEXIST) {\n        free(json_text);\n        pthread_mutex_unlock(&rc->lock);\n        return rc_log_err(RC_ERR_IO, \"mkdir rc->sha256_hex);\n\n    FILE *fp = fopen(file_path, \"w\");\n    if (!fp) {\n        free(json_text);\n        pthread_mutex_unlock(&rc->lock);\n        return rc_log_err(RC_ERR_IO, \"fopen '%s' failed: %s\",\n                          file_path, strerror(errno));\n    }\n\n    if (fwrite(json_text, 1, strlen(json_text), fp)\n        != strlen(json_text)) {\n        fclose(fp);\n        unlink(file_path);\n        free(json_text);\n        pthread_mutex_unlock(&rc->lock);\n        return rc_log_err(RC_ERR_IO, \"fwrite failed\");\n    }\n    fclose(fp);\n\n    /* -------------------- rd_kafka_producev(\n                rk,\n                RD_KAFKA_V_TOPIC(kafka_topic),\n                RD_KAFKA_V_VALUE(rc->sha256_hex, SHA256_HEX_LENGTH),\n                RD_KAFKA_V_END);\n rd_kafka_destroy(rk);\n        }\n    }\n#else\n    UNUSED(kafka_topic);\n#endif\n\n    /* mark finalized */\n    rc->finalized = true;\n    free(json_text);\n    pthread_mutex_unlock(&rc->lock);\n    return RC_OK;\n}\n\n/*\n * rc_get_sha256()\n * Returns the hex-encoded SHA-256 hash after finalize().\n */\nconst char *\nrc_get_sha256(recipe_composer_t *rc)\n{\n    if (!rc || !rc->finalized) return NULL;\n    return rc->sha256_hex;\n}\n\n/* ---------- Internal Helpers ------------------------------------------- */\n\nstatic int\nrc_log_err(int rc, const char *fmt, ...)\n{\n    va_list ap;\n    va_start(ap, fmt);\n    fprintf(stderr, \"[recipe_composer] ERROR: \");\n    vfprintf(stderr, fmt, ap);\n    fprintf(stderr, \"\\n\");\n    va_end(ap);\n    return rc;\n}\n\nstatic End-to-End Minting Data Flow Analysis\n\n### 1.1 Overview\n\nHoloCanvas is a C-based microservice ecosystem that orchestrates the minting, governance, and market evolution of generative NFTs. The services communicate through **Kafka** event streams and **gRPC** contracts, as documented in `ARCHITECTURE.md:27-28`.\n\n### 1.2 Complete Minting Lifecycle\n\nThe minting flow traverses the following services:\n\n```\nUser → Wallet-Proxy → LedgerCore → Consensus → Mint-Factory → Gallery-Gateway\n```\n\n#### Step 1: User Request Entry (Wallet-Proxy)\n\n**File:** `services/wallet_proxy/src/proxy_server.c`\n\nThe user initiates a mint request through the Wallet-Proxy, which provides:\n- Wallet creation using Ed25519 key pairs (`proxy_server.c:258-290`)\n- Transaction signing (`proxy_server.c:332-400`)\n- Transaction broadcast to LedgerCore (`proxy_server.c:402-443`)\n\nThe `handle_tx_broadcast()` function at line 402 calls `ledger_broadcast_transaction()` to forward signed transactions to LedgerCore.\n\n#### Step 2: Transaction Processing (LedgerCore)\n\n**Files:**\n- `services/ledger_core/src/transaction_processor.c`\n- `services/ledger_core/src/block_builder.c`\n- `services/ledger_core/src/consensus_manager.c`\n\n**Transaction Processor (`transaction_processor.c:147-200`):**\n\nThe `tp_process()` function handles incoming transactions through a synchronous validation pipeline:\n1. **Decode** - Deserializes the transaction (`tx_decode()`)\n2. **Signature Verification** - Ed25519 signature verification (`tp_verify_signature()` at line 209-232)\n3. **Nonce Verification** - Anti-replay protection (`tp_verify_nonce()` at line 238-252)\n4. **Funds Verification** - Balance validation (`tp_verify_funds()` at line 257-275)\n5. **State Application** - Mutates ledger state under a **global mutex** (`tp_apply()` at line 288-352)\n\nCritical observation from `transaction_processor.c:286-287`:\n```c\n/* NOTE: called inside the global mutex for now. In production deployments\n   we may employ sharded, fine-grained locks or MVCC to improve throughput. */\n```\n\n**Block Builder (`block_builder.c:147-222`):**\n\nThe block builder runs in a dedicated worker thread that:\n1. Monitors the mempool for pending transactions\n2. Triggers block creation based on:\n   - Transaction count threshold (`cfg.max_txs_per_block`)\n   - Time-based interval (`cfg.max_block_interval_ms`)\n3. Builds a Merkle tree of transactions (`compute_merkle_root()` at line 279-322)\n4. Submits candidate blocks to the consensus engine (`consensus_submit_block()` at line 204)\n\n**Consensus Manager (`consensus_manager.c:297-357`):**\n\nThe consensus manager's worker loop (`_worker_loop()`) processes blocks **sequentially**:\n1. Dequeues a candidate block (`_dequeue_block()`)\n2. **Verifies** the block via pluggable consensus engine (`engine.verify_block()` at line 308)\n3. **Finalizes** state transitions (`engine.finalise_block()` at line 320)\n4. **Commits** to durable storage (`ledger_storage_put_block()` at line 333)\n5. **Publishes** \"ledger.block.finalised\" event to Kafka (`event_bus_publish()` at line 344)\n\n#### Step 3: Consensus Engine (PoS Strategy)\n\n**File:** `services/ledger_core/src/strategies/pos_strategy.c`\n\nThe Proof-of-Stake consensus plugin (`pos_strategy.c`) performs:\n- Validator selection via VRF (`vrf_elect()` at line 427-463)\n- Block verification (`crypto_verify_block()`)\n- Reward distribution (`apply_block_rewards()` at line 472-505)\n\nThe `pos_strategy_handle_block()` function (line 273-312) validates blocks synchronously, verifying signatures and proposer eligibility **for each block**.\n\n#### Step 4: Event Propagation to Mint-Factory\n\n**File:** `services/mint_factory/src/event_handler.c`\n\nThe Mint-Factory's event handler subscribes to Kafka topics and consumes events in a dedicated thread (`run_loop()` at line 201-248):\n- Parses incoming JSON events (`parse_event()` at line 262-319)\n- Dispatches to appropriate handlers based on event type:\n  - `EVT_MINT_REQUEST` → `mint_engine_handle_request()` (line 340)\n  - `EVT_BID` → `mint_engine_handle_bid()` (line 345)\n\n**Key Configuration from `event_handler.c:55-60`:**\n```c\n#define KAFKA_TOPICS \"mint-factory.events\"\n#define KAFKA_CONSUMER_POLL_TIMEOUT_MS 250\n```\n\n#### Step 5: Artifact Generation (Mint-Factory)\n\n**Files:**\n- `services/mint_factory/src/recipe_composer.c`\n- `services/mint_factory/src/artifact_factory.c`\n\n**Recipe Composer (`recipe_composer.c`):**\n- Assembles creative fragments (shader, audio, metadata) into canonical JSON\n- Computes SHA-256 hash for deterministic artifact ID\n- **Synchronously persists** to disk and optionally emits Kafka event\n\n**Artifact Factory (`artifact_factory.c:176-252`):**\n\nThe `af_mint()` function performs:\n1. Fragment validation (`validate_fragments()`)\n2. JSON recipe composition (`compose_recipe_json()`)\n3. SHA-256 ID generation (`sha256_hex()`)\n4. **Synchronous persistence** to LedgerCore (`persist_to_ledger()` at line 219)\n5. Event emission (\"ArtifactMinted\" to `artifact.events` topic at line 238)\n\n#### Step 6: Gallery Update (Gallery-Gateway)\n\n**File:** `services/gallery_gateway/src/query_service.c`\n\nThe query service maintains an LRU cache and federates data from LedgerCore:\n- `gallery_query_get_artifact()` (line 459-510) retrieves artifact state\n- Cache TTL: 5 minutes (`DEFAULT_CACHE_TTL_SEC` at line 52)\n- Falls back to HTTP GET from LedgerCore endpoint\n\n### 1.3 Kafka Topic Configuration\n\n**File:** `config/kafka_topics.json`\n\nKey topics in the minting flow:\n| Topic | Purpose | Partitions |\n|-------|---------|------------|\n| `ledgercore.tx.incoming` | Raw transactions to LedgerCore | 9 |\n| `mint-factory.request` | Minting requests | 12 |\n| `mint-factory.artifact.minted` | Successful mint notifications | 3 |\n| `consensus.block.finalized` | Block finalization events | 3 |\n\n---\n\n## 2. Architectural Bottleneck Identification\n\n### 2.1 Primary Bottleneck: Serial Block Processing in LedgerCore Consensus\n\nThe **primary scalability bottleneck** is the **serial, synchronous block processing** in the LedgerCore consensus layer.\n\n#### Evidence:\n\n**1. Single-threaded Consensus Worker (`consensus_manager.c:297-357`):**\n\n```c\nstatic void *_worker_loop(void *arg)\n{\n    consensus_manager_t *mgr = arg;\n    for (;;) {\n        block_t *blk = _dequeue_block(mgr);  // BLOCKING dequeue\n        if (!blk) break;\n\n        // Step 1: Verify candidate block - SYNCHRONOUS\n        if (!mgr->engine.verify_block(blk, &err_msg)) { ... }\n\n        // Step 2: Finalise - SYNCHRONOUS\n        if (!mgr->engine.finalise_block(blk, new_state_root, &err_msg)) { ... }\n\n        // Step 3: Commit to storage - SYNCHRONOUS\n        if (!ledger_storage_put_block(mgr->storage, blk) || ...) { ... }\n\n        // Step 4: Publish event - only AFTER all above complete\n        event_bus_publish(mgr->event_bus, \"ledger.block.finalised\", blk, sizeof(*blk));\n    }\n}\n```\n\nBlocks are processed **one at a time**, serially. Each block must be fully verified, finalized, and committed before the next block can be processed.\n\n**2. Global Mutex in Transaction Processor (`transaction_processor.c:286-352`):**\n\n```c\nstatic int tp_apply(txp_ctx_t *ctx, const tx_t *tx, uint64_t ts_unix)\n{\n    pthread_mutex_lock(&ctx->mtx);  // GLOBAL LOCK\n\n    /* All ledger mutations happen here under a single lock */\n    ledger_append(ctx->ledger, &entry);\n    ledger_inc_nonce(ctx->ledger, tx->from);\n    ledger_debit(ctx->ledger, tx->from, ...);\n    ledger_credit(ctx->ledger, tx->to, tx->value);\n\n    /* Domain-specific mutations also serialized */\n    switch (tx->type) {\n        case TX_MINT_NFT:\n            artifact_state_on_mint(ctx->ledger, &tx->payload.mint);\n            break;\n        // ...\n    }\n\n    pthread_mutex_unlock(&ctx->mtx);\n}\n```\n\nThe code explicitly acknowledges this limitation in the comment: *\"In production deployments we may employ sharded, fine-grained locks or MVCC to improve throughput.\"*\n\n**3. Per-Block Consensus Overhead (`pos_strategy.c:273-312`):**\n\nEach block requires:\n- Cryptographic signature verification (`crypto_verify_block()`)\n- Validator lookup and state validation\n- VRF-based proposer selection for the next block\n- Reward distribution calculations\n\n**4. Sequential Kafka Event Publishing:**\n\nThe `ledger.block.finalised` event is only published **after** all synchronous operations complete. Downstream services (Mint-Factory, Gallery-Gateway) cannot proceed until this event arrives.\n\n### 2.2 Secondary Bottleneck: Synchronous Artifact Generation\n\nThe Mint-Factory's artifact generation is also synchronous:\n\n**`artifact_factory.c:218-224`:**\n```c\n/* Persist recipe to LedgerCore */\nif (!persist_to_ledger(artifact_id, recipe_str))\n{\n    HC_LOG_ERROR(\"artifact_factory: failed to persist artifact to ledger\");\n    free(recipe_str);\n    return AF_STATUS_ERR_DEPENDENCY;\n}\n```\n\nThis creates a **synchronous round-trip** back to LedgerCore for each mint, compounding the consensus bottleneck.\n\n### 2.3 Impact During Peak Load\n\nDuring \"evolution events\" triggered by Muse-Observer:\n1. A surge of mint requests floods `ledgercore.tx.incoming`\n2. Transactions queue in the mempool waiting for block inclusion\n3. Block builder creates candidate blocks\n4. Consensus processes blocks **one at a time**\n5. Each block finalization creates backpressure\n6. Mint-Factory waits for block finalization events\n7. Gallery-Gateway waits for artifact creation events\n8. Users experience minutes of latency instead of seconds\n\n---\n\n## 3. Proposed Architectural Solution: Batch Aggregation with Off-Chain Pre-Commitment\n\n### 3.1 High-Level Design\n\nTransform the one-by-one transaction model into a **batch aggregation** architecture using an off-chain pre-commitment queue.\n\n```\n                     ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐\n                     │     NEW: Batch Aggregator Service   │\n                     │  (Aggregates mint intents off-chain)│\n                     └────────────────┬────────────────────┘\n                                      │\nUser → Wallet-Proxy → [mint-intent.queue] → Batch Aggregator → LedgerCore\n                           (Kafka)              │                  │\n                                                │                  │\n                                    Merkle Root │                  │\n                                    Commitment  │                  │\n                                                ▼                  ▼\n                                      ┌─────────────────┐   ┌────────────────┐\n                                      │ Batch Storage   │   │ Single Batch   │\n                                      │ (IPFS/S3/Side- │   │ Transaction    │\n                                      │  chain DB)      │   │ On-Chain       │\n                                      └────────┬────────┘   └───────┬────────┘\n                                               │                    │\n                                               └──────┬─────────────┘\n                                                      │\n                                                      ▼\n                                            ┌─────────────────┐\n                                            │  Mint-Factory   │\n                                            │ (Parallel Batch │\n                                            │  Processing)    │\n                                            └────────┬────────┘\n                                                     │\n                                                     ▼\n                                            ┌─────────────────┐\n                                            │ Gallery-Gateway │\n                                            └─────────────────┘\n```\n\n### 3.2 New Data Flow\n\n#### Phase 1: Intent Collection (Off-Chain)\n\n1. User mint requests are published to a new high-throughput Kafka topic: `mint-intent.queue`\n2. A new **Batch Aggregator Service** consumes from this topic\n3. Aggregator collects mint intents for a configurable window (e.g., 2 seconds or 500 intents)\n4. For each batch:\n   - Computes a Merkle tree of all mint intent hashes\n   - Stores the full batch data off-chain (IPFS, S3, or side-chain database)\n   - Records the CID/location reference\n\n#### Phase 2: Batch Commitment (On-Chain)\n\n5. Aggregator submits a **single transaction** to LedgerCore containing:\n   - Merkle root of the batch\n   - Batch size count\n   - Reference to off-chain data (IPFS CID)\n6. LedgerCore processes this **one transaction** through consensus (instead of hundreds)\n7. Block is finalized and `batch.finalized` event is emitted\n\n#### Phase 3: Parallel Processing (Mint-Factory)\n\n8. Mint-Factory receives `batch.finalized` event\n9. Retrieves full batch data from off-chain storage\n10. **Parallelizes** artifact generation across multiple worker threads/processes\n11. Each worker generates artifacts and emits individual `artifact.minted` events\n\n### 3.3 Benefits\n\n| Metric | Current Architecture | Proposed Architecture |\n|--------|---------------------|----------------------|\n| Consensus Transactions | N (one per mint) | 1 (one per batch) |\n| Consensus Throughput | ~10-50 TPS | ~5,000-50,000 mints/batch |\n| Block Finalization Wait | Per-transaction | Per-batch |\n| Mint-Factory Processing | Serial | Parallel |\n| Peak Latency | Minutes | Seconds |\n\n### 3.4 Trade-offs\n\n| Aspect | Trade-off |\n|--------|-----------|\n| **Individual Latency** | Slight increase (~2 seconds) for batching window; individual mints must wait for batch aggregation |\n| **Complexity** | New Batch Aggregator service; off-chain storage dependency; Merkle proof verification |\n| **Data Consistency** | Eventual consistency within batch; users see \"pending\" state until batch finalizes |\n| **Recovery** | More complex failure recovery; need to handle partial batch failures |\n| **Cost** | Additional infrastructure (IPFS/S3); but reduced on-chain storage per-mint |\n| **Atomicity** | Batch commits atomically; individual mint failures must be handled separately |\n\n---\n\n## 4. Key Files and Components Requiring Modification\n\n### 4.1 New Service: Batch Aggregator\n\n**Location:** `services/batch_aggregator/` (new)\n\n| File | Component | Modification |\n|------|-----------|--------------|\n| `src/main.c` | Service entry point | Create Kafka consumer/producer initialization |\n| `src/aggregator.c` | Core aggregation logic | Implement batching window, Merkle tree construction |\n| `src/merkle.c` | Merkle tree implementation | Hash computation, proof generation |\n| `src/storage_client.c` | Off-chain storage | IPFS/S3 client for batch persistence |\n| `include/aggregator.h` | Public interface | Define batch structures, configuration |\n\n### 4.2 LedgerCore Modifications\n\n**Location:** `services/ledger_core/`\n\n| File | Component | Modification |\n|------|-----------|--------------|\n| `src/transaction_processor.c` | `tp_apply()` | Add `TX_BATCH_COMMIT` transaction type handling |\n| `src/transaction_processor.c:326-342` | Switch statement | Add case for batch commit validation |\n| `include/transaction.h` | Transaction types | Add `TX_BATCH_COMMIT` enum value |\n| `src/block_builder.c` | Block assembly | Prioritize batch transactions for fast inclusion |\n| `src/consensus_manager.c:344` | Event publishing | Emit `batch.finalized` event type |\n\n### 4.3 Mint-Factory Modifications\n\n**Location:** Implementation Considerations\n\n### 5.1 Batch Window Tuning\n\nThe batch aggregation window should be configurable:\n- **Time-based:** Aggregate for N milliseconds (e.g., 1000-2000ms)\n- **Count-based:** Aggregate up to M intents (e.g., 500-1000)\n- **Hybrid:** Whichever threshold is reached first\n\n### 5.2 Merkle Proof Verification\n\nFor disputes or individual artifact verification:\n- Store Merkle proofs alongside batch data\n- Allow clients to verify inclusion without full batch retrieval\n\n### 5.3 Failure Handling\n\n- **Partial batch failure:** Log failed mints, continue with successful ones\n- **Storage failure:** Retry with exponential backoff; fallback to synchronous mode\n- **Consensus failure:** Re-submit batch transaction\n\n### 5.4 Migration Strategy\n\n1. Deploy Batch Aggregator as optional parallel path\n2. Route percentage of traffic through batch path (A/B testing)\n3. Monitor latency and throughput metrics\n4. Gradually increase batch path traffic\n5. Deprecate direct mint path once stable\n\n---\n\n## 6. Conclusion\n\nThe HoloCanvas minting bottleneck stems from the **serial, synchronous block processing** in LedgerCore's consensus layer, exacerbated by the global mutex in transaction processing. The proposed batch aggregation architecture addresses this by:\n\n1. **Reducing consensus load** from N transactions to 1 per batch\n2. **Enabling parallel artifact generation** in Mint-Factory\n3. **Decoupling** on-chain commitment from off-chain artifact data\n\nWhile introducing additional complexity and a slight latency increase for individual mints, this architecture can improve peak throughput by **100-1000x**, transforming multi-minute delays into sub-10-second response times during evolution events.\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":18499,"cache_read_input_tokens":91857,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":18499,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"72112e33-ac11-4b77-8129-6d64108407eb","uuid":"f2eb1ad8-9f09-4ae3-a1cd-a1b4c2f11a6e"}

End-to-End Minting Data Flow Analysis\n\n### 1.1 Overview\n\nHoloCanvas is a C-based microservice ecosystem that orchestrates the minting, governance, and market evolution of generative NFTs. The services communicate through **Kafka** event streams and **gRPC** contracts, as documented in `ARCHITECTURE.md:27-28`.\n\n### 1.2 Complete Minting Lifecycle\n\nThe minting flow traverses the following services:\n\n```\nUser → Wallet-Proxy → LedgerCore → Consensus → Mint-Factory → Gallery-Gateway\n```\n\n#### Step 1: User Request Entry (Wallet-Proxy)\n\n**File:** `services/wallet_proxy/src/proxy_server.c`\n\nThe user initiates a mint request through the Wallet-Proxy, which provides:\n- Wallet creation using Ed25519 key pairs (`proxy_server.c:258-290`)\n- Transaction signing (`proxy_server.c:332-400`)\n- Transaction broadcast to LedgerCore (`proxy_server.c:402-443`)\n\nThe `handle_tx_broadcast()` function at line 402 calls `ledger_broadcast_transaction()` to forward signed transactions to LedgerCore.\n\n#### Step 2: Transaction Processing (LedgerCore)\n\n**Files:**\n- `services/ledger_core/src/transaction_processor.c`\n- `services/ledger_core/src/block_builder.c`\n- `services/ledger_core/src/consensus_manager.c`\n\n**Transaction Processor (`transaction_processor.c:147-200`):**\n\nThe `tp_process()` function handles incoming transactions through a synchronous validation pipeline:\n1. **Decode** - Deserializes the transaction (`tx_decode()`)\n2. **Signature Verification** - Ed25519 signature verification (`tp_verify_signature()` at line 209-232)\n3. **Nonce Verification** - Anti-replay protection (`tp_verify_nonce()` at line 238-252)\n4. **Funds Verification** - Balance validation (`tp_verify_funds()` at line 257-275)\n5. **State Application** - Mutates ledger state under a **global mutex** (`tp_apply()` at line 288-352)\n\nCritical observation from `transaction_processor.c:286-287`:\n```c\n/* NOTE: called inside the global mutex for now. In production deployments\n   we may employ sharded, fine-grained locks or MVCC to improve throughput. */\n```\n\n**Block Builder (`block_builder.c:147-222`):**\n\nThe block builder runs in a dedicated worker thread that:\n1. Monitors the mempool for pending transactions\n2. Triggers block creation based on:\n   - Transaction count threshold (`cfg.max_txs_per_block`)\n   - Time-based interval (`cfg.max_block_interval_ms`)\n3. Builds a Merkle tree of transactions (`compute_merkle_root()` at line 279-322)\n4. Submits candidate blocks to the consensus engine (`consensus_submit_block()` at line 204)\n\n**Consensus Manager (`consensus_manager.c:297-357`):**\n\nThe consensus manager's worker loop (`_worker_loop()`) processes blocks **sequentially**:\n1. Dequeues a candidate block (`_dequeue_block()`)\n2. **Verifies** the block via pluggable consensus engine (`engine.verify_block()` at line 308)\n3. **Finalizes** state transitions (`engine.finalise_block()` at line 320)\n4. **Commits** to durable storage (`ledger_storage_put_block()` at line 333)\n5. **Publishes** \"ledger.block.finalised\" event to Kafka (`event_bus_publish()` at line 344)\n\n#### Step 3: Consensus Engine (PoS Strategy)\n\n**File:** `services/ledger_core/src/strategies/pos_strategy.c`\n\nThe Proof-of-Stake consensus plugin (`pos_strategy.c`) performs:\n- Validator selection via VRF (`vrf_elect()` at line 427-463)\n- Block verification (`crypto_verify_block()`)\n- Reward distribution (`apply_block_rewards()` at line 472-505)\n\nThe `pos_strategy_handle_block()` function (line 273-312) validates blocks synchronously, verifying signatures and proposer eligibility **for each block**.\n\n#### Step 4: Event Propagation to Mint-Factory\n\n**File:** `services/mint_factory/src/event_handler.c`\n\nThe Mint-Factory's event handler subscribes to Kafka topics and consumes events in a dedicated thread (`run_loop()` at line 201-248):\n- Parses incoming JSON events (`parse_event()` at line 262-319)\n- Dispatches to appropriate handlers based on event type:\n  - `EVT_MINT_REQUEST` → `mint_engine_handle_request()` (line 340)\n  - `EVT_BID` → `mint_engine_handle_bid()` (line 345)\n\n**Key Configuration from `event_handler.c:55-60`:**\n```c\n#define KAFKA_TOPICS \"mint-factory.events\"\n#define KAFKA_CONSUMER_POLL_TIMEOUT_MS 250\n```\n\n#### Step 5: Artifact Generation (Mint-Factory)\n\n**Files:**\n- `services/mint_factory/src/recipe_composer.c`\n- `services/mint_factory/src/artifact_factory.c`\n\n**Recipe Composer (`recipe_composer.c`):**\n- Assembles creative fragments (shader, audio, metadata) into canonical JSON\n- Computes SHA-256 hash for deterministic artifact ID\n- **Synchronously persists** to disk and optionally emits Kafka event\n\n**Artifact Factory (`artifact_factory.c:176-252`):**\n\nThe `af_mint()` function performs:\n1. Fragment validation (`validate_fragments()`)\n2. JSON recipe composition (`compose_recipe_json()`)\n3. SHA-256 ID generation (`sha256_hex()`)\n4. **Synchronous persistence** to LedgerCore (`persist_to_ledger()` at line 219)\n5. Event emission (\"ArtifactMinted\" to `artifact.events` topic at line 238)\n\n#### Step 6: Gallery Update (Gallery-Gateway)\n\n**File:** `services/gallery_gateway/src/query_service.c`\n\nThe query service maintains an LRU cache and federates data from LedgerCore:\n- `gallery_query_get_artifact()` (line 459-510) retrieves artifact state\n- Cache TTL: 5 minutes (`DEFAULT_CACHE_TTL_SEC` at line 52)\n- Falls back to HTTP GET from LedgerCore endpoint\n\n### 1.3 Kafka Topic Configuration\n\n**File:** `config/kafka_topics.json`\n\nKey topics in the minting flow:\n| Topic | Purpose | Partitions |\n|-------|---------|------------|\n| `ledgercore.tx.incoming` | Raw transactions to LedgerCore | 9 |\n| `mint-factory.request` | Minting requests | 12 |\n| `mint-factory.artifact.minted` | Successful mint notifications | 3 |\n| `consensus.block.finalized` | Block finalization events | 3 |\n\n---\n\n## 2. Architectural Bottleneck Identification\n\n### 2.1 Primary Bottleneck: Serial Block Processing in LedgerCore Consensus\n\nThe **primary scalability bottleneck** is the **serial, synchronous block processing** in the LedgerCore consensus layer.\n\n#### Evidence:\n\n**1. Single-threaded Consensus Worker (`consensus_manager.c:297-357`):**\n\n```c\nstatic void *_worker_loop(void *arg)\n{\n    consensus_manager_t *mgr = arg;\n    for (;;) {\n        block_t *blk = _dequeue_block(mgr);  // BLOCKING dequeue\n        if (!blk) break;\n\n        // Step 1: Verify candidate block - SYNCHRONOUS\n        if (!mgr->engine.verify_block(blk, &err_msg)) { ... }\n\n        // Step 2: Finalise - SYNCHRONOUS\n        if (!mgr->engine.finalise_block(blk, new_state_root, &err_msg)) { ... }\n\n        // Step 3: Commit to storage - SYNCHRONOUS\n        if (!ledger_storage_put_block(mgr->storage, blk) || ...) { ... }\n\n        // Step 4: Publish event - only AFTER all above complete\n        event_bus_publish(mgr->event_bus, \"ledger.block.finalised\", blk, sizeof(*blk));\n    }\n}\n```\n\nBlocks are processed **one at a time**, serially. Each block must be fully verified, finalized, and committed before the next block can be processed.\n\n**2. Global Mutex in Transaction Processor (`transaction_processor.c:286-352`):**\n\n```c\nstatic int tp_apply(txp_ctx_t *ctx, const tx_t *tx, uint64_t ts_unix)\n{\n    pthread_mutex_lock(&ctx->mtx);  // GLOBAL LOCK\n\n    /* All ledger mutations happen here under a single lock */\n    ledger_append(ctx->ledger, &entry);\n    ledger_inc_nonce(ctx->ledger, tx->from);\n    ledger_debit(ctx->ledger, tx->from, ...);\n    ledger_credit(ctx->ledger, tx->to, tx->value);\n\n    /* Domain-specific mutations also serialized */\n    switch (tx->type) {\n        case TX_MINT_NFT:\n            artifact_state_on_mint(ctx->ledger, &tx->payload.mint);\n            break;\n        // ...\n    }\n\n    pthread_mutex_unlock(&ctx->mtx);\n}\n```\n\nThe code explicitly acknowledges this limitation in the comment: *\"In production deployments we may employ sharded, fine-grained locks or MVCC to improve throughput.\"*\n\n**3. Per-Block Consensus Overhead (`pos_strategy.c:273-312`):**\n\nEach block requires:\n- Cryptographic signature verification (`crypto_verify_block()`)\n- Validator lookup and state validation\n- VRF-based proposer selection for the next block\n- Reward distribution calculations\n\n**4. Sequential Kafka Event Publishing:**\n\nThe `ledger.block.finalised` event is only published **after** all synchronous operations complete. Downstream services (Mint-Factory, Gallery-Gateway) cannot proceed until this event arrives.\n\n### 2.2 Secondary Bottleneck: Synchronous Artifact Generation\n\nThe Mint-Factory's artifact generation is also synchronous:\n\n**`artifact_factory.c:218-224`:**\n```c\n/* Persist recipe to LedgerCore */\nif (!persist_to_ledger(artifact_id, recipe_str))\n{\n    HC_LOG_ERROR(\"artifact_factory: failed to persist artifact to ledger\");\n    free(recipe_str);\n    return AF_STATUS_ERR_DEPENDENCY;\n}\n```\n\nThis creates a **synchronous round-trip** back to LedgerCore for each mint, compounding the consensus bottleneck.\n\n### 2.3 Impact During Peak Load\n\nDuring \"evolution events\" triggered by Muse-Observer:\n1. A surge of mint requests floods `ledgercore.tx.incoming`\n2. Transactions queue in the mempool waiting for block inclusion\n3. Block builder creates candidate blocks\n4. Consensus processes blocks **one at a time**\n5. Each block finalization creates backpressure\n6. Mint-Factory waits for block finalization events\n7. Gallery-Gateway waits for artifact creation events\n8. Users experience minutes of latency instead of seconds\n\n---\n\n## 3. Proposed Architectural Solution: Batch Aggregation with Off-Chain Pre-Commitment\n\n### 3.1 High-Level Design\n\nTransform the one-by-one transaction model into a **batch aggregation** architecture using an off-chain pre-commitment queue.\n\n```\n                     ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐\n                     │     NEW: Batch Aggregator Service   │\n                     │  (Aggregates mint intents off-chain)│\n                     └────────────────┬────────────────────┘\n                                      │\nUser → Wallet-Proxy → [mint-intent.queue] → Batch Aggregator → LedgerCore\n                           (Kafka)              │                  │\n                                                │                  │\n                                    Merkle Root │                  │\n                                    Commitment  │                  │\n                                                ▼                  ▼\n                                      ┌─────────────────┐   ┌────────────────┐\n                                      │ Batch Storage   │   │ Single Batch   │\n                                      │ (IPFS/S3/Side- │   │ Transaction    │\n                                      │  chain DB)      │   │ On-Chain       │\n                                      └────────┬────────┘   └───────┬────────┘\n                                               │                    │\n                                               └──────┬─────────────┘\n                                                      │\n                                                      ▼\n                                            ┌─────────────────┐\n                                            │  Mint-Factory   │\n                                            │ (Parallel Batch │\n                                            │  Processing)    │\n                                            └────────┬────────┘\n                                                     │\n                                                     ▼\n                                            ┌─────────────────┐\n                                            │ Gallery-Gateway │\n                                            └─────────────────┘\n```\n\n### 3.2 New Data Flow\n\n#### Phase 1: Intent Collection (Off-Chain)\n\n1. User mint requests are published to a new high-throughput Kafka topic: `mint-intent.queue`\n2. A new **Batch Aggregator Service** consumes from this topic\n3. Aggregator collects mint intents for a configurable window (e.g., 2 seconds or 500 intents)\n4. For each batch:\n   - Computes a Merkle tree of all mint intent hashes\n   - Stores the full batch data off-chain (IPFS, S3, or side-chain database)\n   - Records the CID/location reference\n\n#### Phase 2: Batch Commitment (On-Chain)\n\n5. Aggregator submits a **single transaction** to LedgerCore containing:\n   - Merkle root of the batch\n   - Batch size count\n   - Reference to off-chain data (IPFS CID)\n6. LedgerCore processes this **one transaction** through consensus (instead of hundreds)\n7. Block is finalized and `batch.finalized` event is emitted\n\n#### Phase 3: Parallel Processing (Mint-Factory)\n\n8. Mint-Factory receives `batch.finalized` event\n9. Retrieves full batch data from off-chain storage\n10. **Parallelizes** artifact generation across multiple worker threads/processes\n11. Each worker generates artifacts and emits individual `artifact.minted` events\n\n### 3.3 Benefits\n\n| Metric | Current Architecture | Proposed Architecture |\n|--------|---------------------|----------------------|\n| Consensus Transactions | N (one per mint) | 1 (one per batch) |\n| Consensus Throughput | ~10-50 TPS | ~5,000-50,000 mints/batch |\n| Block Finalization Wait | Per-transaction | Per-batch |\n| Mint-Factory Processing | Serial | Parallel |\n| Peak Latency | Minutes | Seconds |\n\n### 3.4 Trade-offs\n\n| Aspect | Trade-off |\n|--------|-----------|\n| **Individual Latency** | Slight increase (~2 seconds) for batching window; individual mints must wait for batch aggregation |\n| **Complexity** | New Batch Aggregator service; off-chain storage dependency; Merkle proof verification |\n| **Data Consistency** | Eventual consistency within batch; users see \"pending\" state until batch finalizes |\n| **Recovery** | More complex failure recovery; need to handle partial batch failures |\n| **Cost** | Additional infrastructure (IPFS/S3); but reduced on-chain storage per-mint |\n| **Atomicity** | Batch commits atomically; individual mint failures must be handled separately |\n\n---\n\n## 4. The solution document has been written to `/logs/agent/solution.md`.\n\n## Summary of Analysis\n\n### 1. End-to-End Minting Flow\nI traced the complete minting lifecycle through:\n- **Wallet-Proxy** (`proxy_server.c`) - User entry point for transaction signing and broadcast\n- **LedgerCore** - Transaction processing (`transaction_processor.c`), block building (`block_builder.c`), and consensus (`consensus_manager.c`)\n- **Consensus Engine** - PoS strategy (`pos_strategy.c`) for block validation\n- **Mint-Factory** - Event handling (`event_handler.c`) and artifact generation (`artifact_factory.c`, `recipe_composer.c`)\n- **Gallery-Gateway** - Query service (`query_service.c`) for artifact retrieval\n\nThe services communicate via **Kafka** topics (configured in `kafka_topics.json`) and **gRPC** using Protocol Buffers (`holocanvas.proto`).\n\n### 2. Primary Bottleneck Identified\n**Serial, synchronous block processing in LedgerCore's consensus layer:**\n\n1. The consensus manager (`consensus_manager.c:297-357`) processes blocks **one at a time** in a single worker thread\n2. Transaction processor uses a **global mutex** (`transaction_processor.c:286-352`) for all state mutations\n3. Each mint requires a full consensus round-trip before downstream services can proceed\n4.
